### ANDREW RODGER ############################################################

/Rodger_Andrew/
COMMENT: All Andrew's own comments indicated by (AR).

/Rodger_Andrew/Alien_3.sid
COMMENT: "Alien 3 was the most difficult project I worked on - but I loved
         doing it. Probe sent me an audio cassette of the Sega Genesis version
         to convert to the C64. Naturally, I was only allowed the bare minimum
         of free memory, so I had to chuck out all the effects modules in my
         music player to make room for the data (even vibrato had to go). The
         result was some rather flat sounding tunes!" (AR)

/Rodger_Andrew/Carnage.sid
(#3)
  TITLE: Led Storm, Tune #7
 ARTIST: Tim Follin

/Rodger_Andrew/Sky_Runner.sid
COMMENT: "It's called 'Sky Runner' because at the time I loved the magazine
         advertisment artwork for the C64 game of the same name." (AR)

/Rodger_Andrew/Sleepwalker.sid
COMMENT: "Sleepwalker was my first commercial project, and I was nervous as
         hell - I was sure they would reject it!" (AR)

/Rodger_Andrew/Titanic_Blinky.sid
(#18)
  TITLE: Theme from Peter Gunn [from the TV series]
 ARTIST: Henry Mancini

### MARK TAIT ################################################################

/Tait_Mark/
COMMENT: Comments by Mark Tait himself are denoted by (MT).

/Tait_Mark/1943.sid
COMMENT: "1943 was the first commercial game I wrote the music for, and was
         responsible for me being offered the job at Tiertex. The people who
         wrote 1943 were John Prince and Donald ?????? (sorry can't remember
         his last name). They partnered up and started Tiertex in 1987/8, and
         offered me the job as musician. There were only 5 of us in the company
         at that time, and we were writing only for Capcom/US Gold, working
         mainly on arcade conversions." (MT)

/Tait_Mark/Better_Dead_Than_Alien.sid
(#1)
  TITLE: Na Na Hey Hey Kiss Him Goodbye (remix)
 ARTIST: Stem
COMMENT: It is not a straight remix, but it's based on it.
(#5)
  TITLE: Battlestar Galactica [from the TV series]
 ARTIST: Glen A. Larson & Stu Phillips

/Tait_Mark/Black_Tiger.sid
(#1)
  TITLE: Black Tiger
 ARTIST: Tamasan
COMMENT: Converted from the arcade version.
(#2)
  TITLE: Black Tiger
 ARTIST: Tamasan
COMMENT: Converted from the arcade version.
(#3)
  TITLE: Black Tiger
 ARTIST: Tamasan
COMMENT: Converted from the arcade version.

/Tait_Mark/Galaxia_7.sid
COMMENT: "I cannot remember giving my permission for it to be used in a
         computer game. It was a Compunet demo I uploaded in about 1986/7 which
         I can only assume was ripped [...] but I did not receive any payment
         from any sales of it." (MT)

/Tait_Mark/Human_Killing_Machine.sid
(#1)
  TITLE: Comic Bakery, Tune #1
 ARTIST: Martin Galway
COMMENT: The whole music shows a big resemblance to this tune, especially the
         bassline and the arpeggios.
         "I will freely admit to Rob Hubbard and Martin Galway being my biggest
         influences of the time (as far as computer games were concerned), and
         I will not deny at all that their music had a certain influence on me.
         I truly wish however, that no-one thinks that I was ever copying them.
         If any of their sounds or productions in general rubbed off on me, I
         can only hope that they thought of me as a fan, and not as a
         plagarist." (MT)

/Tait_Mark/Last_Crusade.sid
  TITLE: Theme from The Raiders of the Lost Ark [from the movie]
 ARTIST: John Williams
COMMENT: "... for the Last Crusade we got the original John Williams
         handwritten score of the main theme from the film. That's what I
         worked from to produce the music for the games (C64 and pc, Amiga, ST
         etc etc etc). [...] I was quite proud of that, if no one else was."
         (MT)

/Tait_Mark/Protector.sid
COMMENT: "I do remember the music, just not the permission to use it in a
         game." (MT)

/Tait_Mark/Space_Biker.sid
COMMENT: "I do remember the music, just not the permission to use it in a
         game." (MT)

/Tait_Mark/Strider.sid
COMMENT: The same music was used in the sequel, Strider II. Music from the
         arcade version.
         "I left before Strider II was even ever mentioned (I'm sure howere it
         was given the 'inhouse name of ' TOR, although I honestly cannot
         remember what TOR stood for (although I do remeber proucing a killer
         of a soundtrack which was recorded on an Akai X7000,and was, even by
         my standards, probably the best soundtrack thet was ever produced from
         Tiertex TOR is machine)." (MT)

/Tait_Mark/Thunder_Blade.sid
COMMENT: "Thunder Blades music was produced over night. It was decided that a
         demo of a level would be appearing on the cover of Zzap! 64. The
         actual C64 game was not written by Tiertex (although the other
         versions were), it was a guy called Chris someone. As I remember, he
         credited everyone but me!" (MT) (Chris someone = Chris Butler)

### JEROEN TEL/ ##############################################################

  TITLE: After Burner
 ARTIST: Super Sonic Team (SEGA's inhouse band)

/Tel_Jeroen/Bad_Blood_intro_tunes.sid
(#2)
COMMENT: Q: Is this a cover?
         JT: NO! Worldshocking huh? I could actually compose music in the
         classical traditional sence :) It's all COMPOSED by me. More people
         seemed to be wondering if I *really composed Bad Blood*. The answer is
         yes with a capital Y.
         Charles Deenen said it was the "best music I made on c64" when I
         finished it. (I do NOT agree ofcourse, although I must say it's the
         best "filmscore" soundtrack I've done for a game on the c64. I
         actually got some kind of filmscript for it, that's why I could be
         very specific when composing themes for characters and events in the
         game.

         when you finished the game)."
         "Golden Axe was a piece of cake [...]. It took me one day (or should I

/Tel_Jeroen/Kinetix.sid
COMMENT: This game was released under the name of 'Dynamix' in the UK, but
         Kinetix was the original working title.

/Tel_Jeroen/KOUD_HE.sid
COMMENT: This tune was the original prototype for the title theme from Savage.
         Jeroen did this first, sampling the Dutch comedian Harry Vermeegen.
         'Koud he' was his catchphrase and translated it means 'Cold eh?'.
         His most notorious use of this was a Dutch TV programme called 'Pisa'
         (filmed from the leaning tower) with Harry saying his 'Koud He?'
         catchphrase to everyone that went by. Probe Software then heard the
         tune and asked Jeroen if he'd change the samples, and lo and behold
         it became the Savage theme.
         "Some background info. The 'KOUD HE' sample is from a Dutch comedian
         called 'Harry Vermeegen'. It was like a hype saying. I found it a nice
         sample to play around with. A spontanious tune I did just 4 fun." (JT)

/Tel_Jeroen/Navy_Moves.sid
(#2)
COMMENT: "It's just that it SOUNDS like a Hawaiian song. I composed it myself
          though! :)  The slides up have probably be used in a zillion Hawaiian
          songs." (JT)
          Although these tunes were used on 'Mega Phoenix' as well, they were
          used in this game first. Like Army Moves, it was a two part game. The
          reason for the Hawaiian style of music in tune #2 is that the end
          sequence displayed your character on a Hawaiian beach type scene
          complete with a bit of text.

/Tel_Jeroen/Nighthunter.sid
(#1)
COMMENT: " 'Nighthunter' voice is me. The 'laughing' and the 'Allright!' is ME
         and my BROTHER, just before we're about to hit town. That explain the
         silly 'BURP!' and 'AJIIRK' too. My brother was pushing me to go with
         him, so I asked him to sample some voices real fast. (in this case,
         rather silly ones.) This is probably the most 'rushed' music I've ever
         made. (which shows somehow). I like many of the ideas in it, but I
         just had to meet the deadline. No time for quality checks... just...
         PRODUCE and DELIVER. Too bad somehow. It give us a funny compilation
         of brainactivity though :O
         BTW: If you want to know my brother's name: It's 'Kees Tel' (he's an
         awesome composer and musician. He composes almost any imaginable style
         of music and plays a LOT of instruments.)" (All comments by JT.)

/Tel_Jeroen/North_and_South.sid
COMMENT: Some of the jingles are covers. #24 is for instance also in Children
         Songs and Frogger.
         Music #1 is clearly a medley of famous confed songs, but it was
         written by Charles Callet for the Amiga version of the game and then
         converted by Jeroen Tel.
         "The tunes were converted by me from the Amiga version. That's all I
         can say." (JT)

/Tel_Jeroen/Outrun_Europa.sid
COMMENT: "To make a complex story short: BEFORE I had to make the 'TURBO
         OUTRUN' music, I was asked to do the OUTRUN EUROPE music. OUTRUN
         EUROPE was never finished (at least not the first version). I don't
         know why/when/where/how.
         Then Turbo Outrun was made. (you know the music, etc.) One of the two
         title-tunes (not the 1.2.3. hit-it! one) was actually composed for
         Outrun Europe.
         OUTRUN EUROPA in 1990 is a totally different game from the old
         unfinished, never released game in 1988/1989." (All comments from JT)
         Tunes #3, #5, and #7 are buggy and fall out of synch at around the
         1:20 mark. This is how they actually sound in the original game, too.
(#1)
 ARTIST: Super Sonic Team (SEGA's inhouse band)
COMMENT: From the arcade version of "Out Run".
(#2)
 ARTIST: Super Sonic Team (SEGA's inhouse band)
COMMENT: From the arcade version of "Out Run".

/Tel_Jeroen/Outrun_Europe_levels.sid
COMMENT: These three tunes were prototypes for a US Gold/Probe game 'Outrun
         Europe'. However, the game was never completed and the programming
         team involved went on to do Turbo Outrun instead. This was also a
         different game from Outrun Europa, released in 1991. The first two
         tunes cover the arcade version.

/Tel_Jeroen/Turbo_Outrun.sid
COMMENT: "Turbo Outrun took about a month all together. It was a lot of fun,
         but I recomposed every tune about three times to get it perfect. I
         still think it is one of the best audio tracks I ever did on the C64."
         "One nice, very nice note: 'Turbo Outrun' (the music) has won the:
         'Best music on 8bit computer 1989' award (at the PCW show (now ECTS)
         in London. :D Too bad I had to find out later. US Gold received the
         actual award. Bastards! I wasn't even at the scene when it was
         presented. :( " (JT)

### Rambones #################################################################

/TST/Rambones/
COMMENT: Rambones' own comments are denoted by (Rambones).
         "These C64 songs were made from 28. December 1986 to 12. September
         1988. They were all written in Chris Hülsbeck's Soundmonitor, and some
         in Rockmonitor 2/3 by OPM/Dutch USA-Team, which is a modified version
         of Chris's original editor. Without this editor, I wouldn't be where I
         am today. So once and for all: thank you Chris! for giving me the
         opportunity." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/1st_Sphere.sid
COMMENT: "A shitty Rockmon piece I couldn't get any system into. But it has
         some cool things after all." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Amiga_Noid.sid
COMMENT: "From my last demo of the same name. I was tired of not being able to
         create some decent drums in soundmon, I was tired of my C64 and
         generally this can be heard in the tune. Again I try to make some
         melody with my 'synth' violin to compensate for the lack of rhythm."
         (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Apolloguys.sid
COMMENT: "One of my personal favourites, despite the fact that I used it in an
         apology demo to Zetrex 2005. My good friends Wizz & Kaze demanded me
         to do this demo in order to stop the war. To compensate for my loss of
         face, I filled the screen with my name in sprites, dancing around so
         that you only would remember 2 things after having seen the demo: the
         music and the name RAMBONES. :) What a hoax, Kaze got his software
         from me, and me and TSN visited Wizz every weekend. There's some
         really nice filter in this one, so try a C64." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Blue_Monday.sid
  TITLE: Blue Monday [from Substance 1987]
 ARTIST: New Order
COMMENT: "Well, like any other teenager with a C64 and some talent, I just had
         to try. I completely forgot the characteristic drums from the
         original. Nothing more to say, the original will always be the best
         (the one by Anthony Crowther) :-)" (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Broken_Mind.sid
COMMENT: "One of my S.I.D. tunes. S.I.D. was my attempt to conquer the
         softwarehouse like Jeroen and Charles. I did this while learning how
         to use my Amiga." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Digital_Dream.sid
COMMENT: "Not really a melody, just some of the COOLEST filtereffects you'd
         ever hear. Used for a short contact note. Do try this on a C64."
         (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Do_The_Mip.sid
COMMENT: "Ha, the joke of my life! Charles Deenen sought musicians who used
         soundmonitor and were into coding. That was me, so I sent him this
         tune, nicely wrapped in an intro. That didn't help any :) By that time
         Noisy Pillars was far over most people's heads anyway." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Dont_Be_Late.sid
COMMENT: "For what ? Well, the Amiga had given me a 'pop' in the head. This is
         actually a song, but I can't find my text anymore.. damn.."
         (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Drums_At_Night.sid
COMMENT: "Just trying to see if I can make some 'real' drums... but no.."
         (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Fairytales.sid
COMMENT: "Another favourite of mine. This was my attempt to imitate some of Tim
         Follin's style. I didn't succeed at that, but I got a wild melody,
         sort of telling a story - that's why the lead is so variable. Actually
         most of my C64 music has an always developing lead, like there's
         endless things to tell." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/First_Rockmon.sid
COMMENT: "Just like it says.. my first Rockmon tune. Now I finally had some
         decent drums, leaving a channel free for more effects. The drums plays
         exactly what I could play live on a real drumset :)" (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Freeze_On.sid
COMMENT: "I really wanted to kick the shit out of the drums, but instead I
         fucked it up by getting to melodious. It get's pretty wild at the end,
         overdrive saw and strange sounds. At 3:30 there's some action."
         (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Guitar_Flan.sid
COMMENT: "Get this over into your C64 now! :) I thought that Tim Follin's
         guitar in Led Storm was too good, and wondered how the hell he did it.
         So, after working for hours, I found some good sounds and was lucky
         with the filter. This guitar really sounds like a distorted guitar!
         and no samples." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/I_Like_Choroid.sid
COMMENT: "That's exactly what I did, and I still do. He made some cool sounds,
         and offcourse I ripped them. I've been searching for you Jonathan for
         like say 8 years, where did you go ? :) I originally put Martin
         Galway's 'Game Over' drums on this tune, but I couldn't convert the
         sampleroutine, and now I don't care, course it was quite noisy anyway.
         :) Just to remind you, try looking in your Choroid dir - and load
         'Choroid's Back!'." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Indian_Walk.sid
COMMENT: "My 2nd or 3rd tune. It's supposed to sound like it's called :) really
         CRAZY sounds, and I like it! It was missing for several years, because
         the disk was broken, but then I found Hagar's old disks, and he had it
         :) Uoh I like these crazy sounds. C64 preferred." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Intrusic.sid
COMMENT: "Means: intro-music. :) Not even a melody, just some tap-dancing."
         (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Jarre_Dreams.sid
COMMENT: "Well, not much Jarre here, but that's the way it goes... This is a
         typical 'Rambones' synth violin yawn yawn long melody. The (in)famous
         sound I refer to was originally ripped from MAD'69 's 'Mixable' demo
         from Scoop. I got stuck with this sound, probably because I played the
         violin for real, and I just couldn't shake it off. :) Again, I
         compensated in the demo, by splatting my name all over the screen."
         (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Like_Galway.sid
COMMENT: "Many have tried, and tried, to imitate Martin's wonderful phasing and
         trance sounds, and we have all failed miserably. :) Anyway, this is
         one of my favourites also, It simply has a bit of atmosphere, maybe
         even a touch of Wizball (which was the general idea). This really
         needs filter! At this point, I had splatted my name all over so many
         demos, that people began to think there was a group called 'The
         Rambones'... I had to write a looong scrolltext, with this music in
         the background, slaying the 'lamers'. :)" (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Maybe_Its_Final.sid
COMMENT: "I was referring to the fact that my Amiga demanded more and more of
         me. So I forgot the melody... The tunes even sounds sad, like I'm
         crying. Actually I think I was..." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Mixed_Emotions.sid
COMMENT: "That's exactly what my emotions were as I used this tune in my last
         demo, on the last screen. It was the Dexion-party in 1988. I had
         released Apolloguys the same day, and I had bought my Amiga 500 only 2
         weeks before coming to this party. The first screen featured 'Like
         Galway', and me writing a lot of text about who and what 'Rambones'
         was. The middle screen was the 'Amiga Noid' screen, with a small
         picture of an Amiga, and a scroll telling that TST would soon rule it.
         The last one, with this music, was a 10 point list of things about the
         C64 that bored the hell out of me. Things like colorbars, sprites
         moving in sinus, Hubbard alike drums/routines, all with the same sound
         and generally everything that was in at that time. For me it was sad
         to see a large variety of composers using more or less the same
         routine, leaving the music in a grey state. I admire the sound of Fred
         Gray, Martin Galway, Rob Hubbard, David Whittaker, Ben Daglish and
         Anthony Crowther, and any other programmer that built it all up from
         scratch." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Multiple_Dreams.sid
COMMENT: "The seed had been planted. After releasing my first music-dedicated
         demo, I had so many new dreams and desires, because now I had finally
         made some music, all by myself. That's kind of the reason for the
         title of this. I don't remember the name, but this piece was inspired
         by a Depeche Mode song." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Negative.sid
COMMENT: "I don't know what to say about this... It was just made sometime, and
         never used for anything. Anyway, it has a nice theme I think..."
         (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Never_Man.sid
COMMENT: "Another attempt to make those pulsedrums work.. but no... :)"
         (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/No_Mercy_Theme.sid
  TITLE: Mission of Mercy
 ARTIST: Peter Clarke
COMMENT: "A sort of a try to remake a really great tune by Peter Clarke with
         the same name." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Nu_Er_Den_Sgu_Helt_Gal.sid
COMMENT: "I just wanted to see if I could make a special kind of music that we
         call 'suppe steg og is' here in Denmark. The kind of music you hear at
         family parties, weddings etc. I used it in a special note/greeting
         demo for my contacts, and wrote that it was a Galway tune... Some
         believed me. :)" (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Oceanic.sid
COMMENT: "This needs filter! (C64)... As I saw that Jonathan Dunn continued to
         make music for Ocean, I thought that maybe I had a chance also, so I
         sent this tune to the company. I never got a reply. :) well, I didn't
         really care anyway because I now was using Soundtracker every day, all
         day long. My newfound inspiration: SLL .. :) After having made my
         first few Soundtracker-modules, I sent a demo with some musics and a
         picture to Hewson. They replied: your music is quite good, but we
         cannot accept you as a graphics-artist, the picture lacks all kind of
         inspiration. Arrrghh.... Their music in their games sucked bigtime
         back then..." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Ode_To_Banana_V1.sid
COMMENT: "Everybody old enough knows the 'Ode To Martin' by MC/Dutch USA-Team.
         One of my good contacts, and a very musical one to was Banana/The
         Electronic Knights. He had already made more than 30 tunes when I
         first laid my hands on Soundmon. I didn't know at all how to use this
         program, but I could hear from Chris' and Banana's music that it
         definitely was the way to go. So I asked Banana for some help. He sent
         me more than 10 handwritten pages, how to type in this bass and use
         the program. So, it was my first tune ever. As on any occasion:
         Banana, if you see this, write to me!" (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Ode_To_Banana_V2.sid
COMMENT: "This is much better, I'd even say good. :) The sounds are Banana's
         'Rammax' sounds. Nothing much to say. I love this tune, hope you do
         to." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Ode_To_Laxity.sid
COMMENT: "Well, I met Laxity the first time when he visited Wizz, to code some
         demos. He was very into using Rob's routine, and slowly doing his own
         player. In a transition period he had doing this, he tried
         Soundmonitor, and, not to my surprise, offcourse he was much better
         than I. So, what do you do ? Offcourse, another 'Ode to...' :) I kinda
         like this, as did he, but there's hardly any bass in it, because I try
         (again) to make some drums.,, One more thing: the sounds were made by
         Laxity. :)" (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Resolute.sid
COMMENT: "We are in the final chapter of my C64 days. I was composing /
         drawing / programming / writing, all alone on my final demo: Zound
         Muzak #4. I had just learned FLD, 8-9 months after everybody else, but
         at least I didn't just rip it. I started on several tunes at the same
         time, to try and get them all to sound as they came from one whole,
         like Mike Oldfield does. This was the first one, but I never finished
         ZM4, I was too bored after all." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Sad_and_Bad.sid
COMMENT: "It got its name after the final save. :) A bended bass does some
         funny work, but that's about it." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Satellite.sid
COMMENT: "Used in the first of our 2 Satellite demos, this is a genuine piece
         of early techno style. Not much melody, instead some pumping drums."
         (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Scary_Dreams.sid
COMMENT: "Nothing scary here. I think it got the name because of the
         atmosphere. Do go to a C64 with this! The main force in this tune is
         that I use all 3 voices for melody and no stupid drums. It almost
         sounds as if I designed the tune? No I didn't, almost anything just
         came out by itself." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Shadows_in_Pain.sid
COMMENT: "The bassline was supposed to set a frame for some sixties style, but
         listen what happens later... :) No no, I think this is just a 10 min.
         worksong." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Sound_Shocker.sid
COMMENT: "A quite quiet and slow tune. this NEEDS filter (C64)!! The sounds and
         the quiet melody is what this is all about. I made it at a visit to
         one of my friends, trying to teach him about using Soundmon. It was
         made in less than half an hour, then I had to go on to another friend.
         I was so busy, driving around on my moped, dumping demos/games, and
         then on to the next. Finally, every evening, I would end up at TSN's
         place, and our democoding-session could begin, and last until it was
         time to go to school... :)" (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Space_Alien.sid
COMMENT: "A crazy tune! Used in another WAR demo. :) First screen of 'Who Said
         War ???'. Remember ? The scroll where I slagged Laxity as hard as I
         could, even though I love him for his wonderful music." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Sunshine.sid
COMMENT: "Hmmm.. another drum-experiment perhaps... This isn't finished. It's
         hardly a melody. :)" (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/To_A_Z_er.sid
COMMENT: "I made this for one of my favourite contacts: Wallie/Z-Circle. He was
         one of the few who openly said that he liked my tunes, so I made this
         for him. It could have been better. There's some kind of 'View To a
         Kill' theme, over this whole thing... (hint)" (Rambones)
  TITLE: A View to a Kill (0:31-0:53)
 ARTIST: Duran Duran
  TITLE: A View to a Kill (1:25-1:39)
 ARTIST: Duran Duran

/TST/Rambones/Trombones.sid
COMMENT: "Well, a typical 'Rambones' tune. It has some great waves and echos,
         and one more thing: after 1:10 in comes the trombone - it sounds just
         like a real one, and no samples. A wonderful coincidence made by the
         workings of Soundmonitor. Again, the 'Mixable' sound is here."
         (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Unbeliever.sid
COMMENT: "Just trying to make some long smooth, dark sounds. There's something
         'Rolling' here. :) Or should I say 'Still Rolling'... Hehe, not much
         of a melody." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Vendetta.sid
COMMENT: "From the demo where I taught Kaze how to rip 'Rainbow Dragon'. It's
         one of those '1 melody in each channel' songs." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/When_A_Master.sid
COMMENT: "Needs filter (C64). Has a nice rhythm and some great deep sounds. If
         only I could have made a more variable bass... But, you learn as you
         go along. :)" (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Who_Said_War.sid
COMMENT: "Undefinable 'Rambones' style. Heh... In this demo I totally slagged
         Kaze for being a lousy composer, because he was. Then one day, he made
         'The Force' and his 'New Limits' introtune. Sad that he put that tune
         on an empty screen, when everybody wanted to see the next, the
         unbelievable fullscreen sideborder-scroll with rasters and
         music+samples. If you have his tunes, then try listening to them."
         (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Zound_Muzak_1.sid
COMMENT: "My first 'music only' dedicated demo. Banana had his IBM Music, and I
         wanted my own series. This was it. I made this in a few hours, and had
         to stop myself. I couldn't stop playing! Because I had just discovered
         the 'record' function in Soundmon. It stayed with me for the rest of
         the C64 days; I played the lead in on my brown keyboard." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Zound_Muzak_2-Part_1.sid
COMMENT: "Another version of 'Sound Shocker'. Better with filter, much better."
         (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Zound_Muzak_2-Part_2.sid
COMMENT: "It's reverse! - was its first name. Listen and hear why. :) One of my
         favourites. At the time I made this, it was the happiest time of all.
         Playing Delta every day, for one purpose and one purpose only: the
         music! Sure we had ripped it, but it just wasn't the same, it never
         will be. Come back Rob!..." (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Zound_Muzak_3.sid
COMMENT: "Maybe my finest piece. 3 tunes in one module. 2 of them with
         Rockmonitor drums. The tunes somehow fit well together, because they
         use the same sounds. Notice I learned some more bass here. :) And
         banging those drums! The drums in the last tune were kind of snatched
         from a Matt Gray tune... hehe" (Rambones)

/TST/Rambones/Zound_Muzak_IV.sid
COMMENT: "This is just the intro tune to a demo that was never finished. I
         stole Kaze's NL-bass and tried my way. Strange result I think..."
         (Rambones)

### ELA ######################################################################

/VARIOUS/ELA/
COMMENT: Comments by ELA himself are denoted by (x,y ELA). x is a number that
         shows ELA's personal ranking of a given tune (9 being the best), and y
         is a number that shows how much a given tune was used (0=never
         released, 9=used by many).

/VARIOUS/ELA/Carl_I_Hagen.sid
COMMENT: "Made with the newer Future Composers by BeastieBoys etc. as a member
         of Suppliers.
         Shorty. Cool though!" (7,1 ELA)

/VARIOUS/ELA/Choir_inspired_stuff.sid
COMMENT: "Made with Megastyle's editor. Quit Cosine, and got word from
         Megastyle that they wanted me as a member making tons of
         intro/demo-tunes. I got their editor but they never used ANY of my
         music. Their player was based on JB/MON's Stormlord tunes. I decided
         to join my local friends in the Norway/Finland/Germany-based group
         Antimon. Most of my music had been used by them anyway.
         A somewhat choir-like tune!" (2,0 ELA)

/VARIOUS/ELA/Cirkus_Death.sid
COMMENT: "Made with the newer Future Composers by BeastieBoys etc. as a member
         of of Cosine (English guys). Cosine had their own music division, but
         never had any intensions of including me in it and letting me use
         their routine!
         It's a rotten tune, with lots of bass/filter and no melody." (5,4 ELA)

/VARIOUS/ELA/Complete_solo.sid
COMMENT: "Made with the newer Future Composers by BeastieBoys etc. as a member
         of Suppliers.
         Ok! But the ending is awful!" (6,0 ELA)

         of Suppliers.
         Made for the Errors, but they didn't use it." (7,0 ELA)

         music. Their player was based on JB/MON's Stormlord tunes. I decided

/VARIOUS/KB/F_A_K_E-End_music_GameBoy.sid
COMMENT: "This was the background zak for the end scroller. As this end
         scroller was NOT faked, I decided to use only plain, non-modulated,
         non-filtered square waves... this is why I called this "GameBoy
         version", it simply sounds like sum Super Mario or stuff end tune :)"
         (KB)

/VARIOUS/KB/F_A_K_E-The_End_remixed.sid
  TITLE: F.A.K.E. End Music
 ARTIST: Tammo Hinrichs (KB)

/VARIOUS/KB/First_25Hz_tune_half-speed.sid
COMMENT: "As one of our members had coded a demo part which sucked such an
         amount of time that it was impossible to have a music routine running
         every frame, I was forced to compose a zak which ran at a speed of
         25Hz instead of 50. Pity-ful, this part was never released but it was
         a nice experiment how to cope with all these drum fx when you can't
         really use waveform tables." (KB)

/VARIOUS/KB/Who_Cares-End_part.sid
COMMENT: ":-| Nobody really got the idea, but this was intended as a "remix"
         version of the themes I used in the different demo parts... oh my god.
         Everyone liked it but nobody recognized the melodies... gnrf... btw.

         good on the new SID-Chip. Well, try these Filtersetting in SIDPLAY:
         Parameter 1: <......*>

         Parameter 3: <*......> (This is not the default setting!!!)" (NBA)

COMMENT: "My very last song I ever made on a Computer (till 98-10-17).
         It was composed on a big Bass-Amp, so play it also on a big one,
         you will miss (=not hear) the deep bass. On SIDPLAY its hard to
         let the Hi-Hat sound like on a real C64, try this:
         - Set fiters to default
         - Move Parameter 2 two clicks from the left side." (NBA)

### Odi ######################################################################

/VARIOUS/Odi/
COMMENT: Odi's own comments are denoted by (Odi).

/VARIOUS/Odi/A_Groovy_Night.sid
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         Sascha and I made it a night. Sleep, what's that? By that time, I had
         figured out what number to add to the filter byte to get roughly the
         same sound as with normal filters." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Ancient_Property.sid
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         One of my longest tunes -- and one of my best! The first part is
         partly inspired by J.S.Bach and Jeroen Tel, the second part mostly by
         Drax, being rather short (out of inspiration, just up'n'down the blues
         scale). Uptonogood (don't remember the group, sorry) from Australia
         used this music in his mag 'The Link'." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Autumn_Beach.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Soedesoft's SoundMaster." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Bachs_Domain.sid
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         Inspired by J.S.Bach again, I composed this piece as another magsound
         for 'Genetic Dreams' by Trinomic Boozeline. Willy/Presence favored
         this piece a lot (he himself liking Bach, too). I chose the name with
         a sidelook to Sascha's piece 'Drax' Domain'. This piece consists of a
         few different parts which I just lined up one after another, as I was
         unable to come up with some interludes." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Back_To_The_Future_III.sid
  TITLE: Back to the Future III - Main Title [from the movie]
 ARTIST: Alan Silvestri
COMMENT: "Done with Soedesoft's SoundMaster.
         This is a remake of the title tune from the game. Hawk had asked me to
         do it." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Baroque_Parting.sid
COMMENT: "This tunes had been composed with the full player in mind (an loaded
         into the editor). That player uses a slight de-tuning for voice #2 and
         #3 in order to achieve a fuller sound. In addition, the instruments
         make use of pulse width modification.
         For the 'zero page version' of this piece, I had cut down the player
         to almost nothing in order to get a real short file with as little
         raster time as possible. During that step, I removed the routine for
         the pulse modification and increased the detuning of voice #2 and #3
         instead, resulting in a slightly different (and slightly less nice)
         sound." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Baroque_Parting_Zero_Page.sid
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         I wrote this piece to express my feelings about a freshly broken
         relationship with my (former, sigh) girlfriend. As we just had
         analyzed fugues in music class, I wrote a fugue (in c-minor) as well.
         There is a zeropage version of this tune that saves even more
         rastertime and memory." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Bass_Fever.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Chris Hülsbeck's Soundmonitor.
         What a shame that I couldn't make a real FAT bassdrum!" (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Chips_In_Music.sid
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         I originally had planned to write a techno tune (without too much
         success, as you can hear). It's one of my personal favorites." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Chords_Of_Endlessness.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Soedesoft's SoundMaster.
         A magsound for the Alcoholics. I don't know if they actually used it."
         (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Cool_Is_Fool.sid
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         Out of inspiration for any new music, I was listening to some of my
         old tunes and started to play around with the old 'Groovy Night'. The
         result was this funny little thing. Listen to the portamento with the
         arpeggio (the drop-down)!" (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Cowboy_Song_2.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Soedesoft's SoundMaster.
         In the big band I played the trumpet in at that time, we used to play
         'Cowboy Song' by Fred Lipsius. I couldn't exactly play it properly,
         but I like it very much nevertheless. So I took its name for this tune
         (which is the only thing these two pieces have in common)." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Dancing_A-Head.sid
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         A funny thing for Trance's diskmag 'A-Head', inspired by the music to
         'Moons' by Drax." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Dream_In_Space.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Chris Hülsbeck's Soundmonitor.
         Again a tune my trumpet teacher liked a lot! This tune was publicly
         performed (err... played from tape) at a performance of his trumpet
         class I was a pupil in. I was very proud of the drums I had squeezed
         out of little Soundmonitor." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Eternal_Sadness.sid
  TITLE: Eternal Sadness
 ARTIST: Traditional
COMMENT: "Done with Soedesoft's SoundMaster.
         A schoolmate had asked me to arrange this traditional funeral march
         for him. I did my best, but in the end he didn't really like the
         result he came to hear. =:-(" (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Fuer_Claudia.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Chris Hülsbeck's Soundmonitor.
         I wrote this tune and the previous one on the same afternoon in order
         to impress a girl from dancing class (without success, alas). But at
         least it was fun discussing politics with her on the phone." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Genetic_Jazz.sid
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         I wrote this piece for Galen/Trinomic (Trinomic Boozeline) for his mag
         'Genetic Dreams' (my favorite mag) as an apology: I had promised to do
         a few conversions for their demo but didn't get them finished in time.
         So they had to use non-exclusive music." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Happy_Birthday.sid
  TITLE: Happy Birthday
 ARTIST: Mildred J. Hill
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         Just a another crappy conversion of this traditional birthday song.
         Too many wrong chords." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Happy_Cadaver.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Chris Hülsbeck's Soundmonitor.
         A play on words with the German word for Corpus Christi Day
         ('Fronleichnam')." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Henry_Level_1.sid
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         This swingy thing was composed for the Jump'n'Run 'Henry' by Peter
         Schwarzfischer (Sky/Masters' Design Group). My first Swing tune for a
         long time (since the 'Underground-Rock'). As it seems, I will still
         compose a different piece for Level 1, as the game has changed its
         mood from funny to more Fantasy-like and gloomy. This music is rather
         old anyway." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Jingle_from_the_Lenor_adve.sid
  TITLE: Lenor advert theme
 ARTIST: <?>
COMMENT: "Done with Future Composer II.
         I didn't like this editor at all (and didn't understand it in full,
         either). An acquaintance from the scene (Doc Holliday or someone from
         Presence ) asked me to convert this music from the 'Lenor' TV ad to
         C64." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Laser_Beat.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Chris Hülsbeck's Soundmonitor." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Lax_Up.sid
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         Inspired by some tune by Laxity (and with some of its instruments,
         ripped by hand). In English, the 'Lax' Up' is pronounced exactly the
         same as the German translation of 'lick it off' (leck's ab). =:-> "
         (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Load_The_Power.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Soedesoft's SoundMaster.
         Hawk/Presence once asked me to compose a short tune for a loader. As
         far as I know, this music has never been used with a loader, though."
         (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Lords_of_Synthesis.sid
COMMENT: "Done with my first own music player.
         Having read about the great features of Soundmonitor, I got fed up
         with the limits of The Music System (TMS) and the music commands from
         BASIC V7.0. So I wrote my own soundplayer. That thing ate memory and
         rastertime like I drink tea and used a notation similar to the BASIC
         commands. But -- it worked and had cool *filters*! I planned to use it
         in a Snake-like game I was coding (Crowding Crawlers), but the game
         made heavy use of raster interrupts and color effects and thus would
         not work with the music (rastertime ranging from $03 to $80 or so...).
         I never finished the game anyway. But my trumpet teacher liked the
         tune _very_ much! =:-> " (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Lunatic_Trashcan.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Soedesoft's SoundMaster." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Mega_Put.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Oliver Blasnik's Romuzak." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Melt_Your_Brain.sid
  TITLE: Piano Sonata in C (KV 545) (0:00-0:06)
 ARTIST: Wolfgang Amadeus Mozart
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         A 4-time techno tune (my best one of this kind so far) which I wrote
         together with a good friend of mine, Ingo Meyer. While we were
         composing, the speaker box fell off the shelf due to one extremely
         loud bass-kick (no kidding!). This piece contributed to the music
         competition at the Data-Live '93 in Dessau, winning the 2nd place.
         Possibly it would have made the 1st if they had had good big speakers
         instead of that tiny thing in their video beamer! =:-( " (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Mothers_Little_Arpeggio.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Oliver Blasnik's Romuzak.
         I came up with the name as my mother didn't like the sound of the
         arpeggios at all." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Musical_News.sid
  TITLE: Tagesschau [from the German TV news] (0:00-0:14)
 ARTIST: <?>
COMMENT: "Done with Soedesoft's SoundMaster.
         Asterix/Victory had asked me to convert the jingle from the German TV
         news 'Tagesschau' for the intro to his diskmag. (In this intro, you
         could see a TV news announcer.) Sascha/DOS/MDG liked this tune very
         much and said to me, grinning: 'I think you've listened to Maniacs of
         Noise far too much!'" (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Nameless_Horror.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Soedesoft's SoundMaster.
         What a horror: I couldn't think of any sensible name for this piece of
         music!" (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Oedipus.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Chris Hülsbeck's Soundmonitor." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Peaceful_Beauty.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Oliver Blasnik's Romuzak.
         I wrote this tune and the previous one for Presence, both on the same
         weekend (I obviously felt that I had to become a little more active)."
         (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Pocket_Funk.sid
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         I merely composed this piece to show that with a special version of my
         CSS player, it is possible to achieve low rastertime of about $10
         (like Music Assembler/Voicetracker, JCH Player V19 or A.K.'s player)."
         (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Raped_SID.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Oliver Blasnik's Romuzak.
         A guy from Red Sector Inc. asked me to compose a lame-sounding cool
         tune (whatever he might exactly have thought of) for an intro of their
         second group, Ultimate (the section for crap releases)." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Rhythm_Of_Life.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Chris Hülsbeck's Soundmonitor." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Rock_Around_The_Block.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Chris Hülsbeck's Soundmonitor.
         Again a (rather obvious) play on words. At that time, I used to write
         either Rock'n'Roll style tunes or the usual 'cool' style sidtunes with
         mainly minor scales." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Rumour_Land.sid
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         This piece was composed at the Data Live '93 for the party report by
         TRL Designs ('The Real Lunatic'). I had a very nasty back-ache after
         the piece was finished." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Session_Tune.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Soedesoft's SoundMaster.
         I composed this piece at the Security group Meeting in December 1991
         in Mannheim. Security (Demons of Sound [DOS] being part of it) was my
         second group at that time. With this tune, I started a new approach to
         composing on the C64, very much Vibrants-like: start with a groovy
         bassline and write the lead voice last, using a lot of blues scale."
         (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Sonlasarka_Dunti.sid
COMMENT: "Done with Soedesoft's SoundMaster.
         This name was supposed to sound Hungarian. My trumpet teacher (who is
         Hungarian) knew better, though. But he liked that tune a lot. I
         composed it for Asterix/Victory as a music for his diskmag." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Trance_Introzak.sid
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         Rather out of inspiration...just a little music for the intro by
         Trance." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Underground-Rock.sid
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         The first tune to be packed with the packer of the CSS! It wrote it
         for the addymag 'Underground' by Presence." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Uranium_Dioxide.sid
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         My first finished attempt to write some techno. I love these harmless
         intros that are composed in an entirely different style than the rest
         of the piece! At the time I composed this piece, we had just learned
         how to write 3- and 4-voiced choir arrangements at school. For
         X-Radical of Nirvana." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Vagabonds_Groove.sid
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         Ok, ok, this tune _is_ heavily inspired by the first music from Dutch
         Breeze, composed by Jeroen Tel. I even hand-ripped the snare drum and
         the bass sound from that piece. Anyway, I wrote this tune for
         Tardox/Vagabonds (now a member of Smash Designs) for his demo, a
         contribution to a demo competition by the magazine '64'er'." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Wind_of_Change.sid
  TITLE: Wind of Change [from Crazy World]
 ARTIST: Scorpions
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         Doc Holliday/Techno Ind. had asked me to do a conversion of this song
         (by The Scorpions) for a coop-demo against racism." (Odi)

/VARIOUS/Odi/Winter_from_The_Four_Seaso.sid
  TITLE: Winter, from The Four Seasons (RV297)
 ARTIST: Antonio Vivaldi
COMMENT: "Done with the Cyberlogic Sound Studio... made by me and Sascha/MDG!.
         A conversion of 'The Winter' from Vivaldi's 'Four Seasons', which I
         ported to the C64 for Domino/Security, an old friend on mine." (Odi)

### Zaw Productions ##########################################################

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/
COMMENT: All comments and information has been submitted by Warren Pilkington
         of Zaw Productions. For the full Zaw Productions tune info, read the
         Zaw Guide at http://www.users.globalnet.co.uk/~wazzaw/zawguide.html
         All Warren's comments are indicated by (WP).

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/12_Days_Of_Christmas.sid
  TITLE: The Twelve Days of Christmas
 ARTIST: Traditional

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/92_9294_Remix.sid
  TITLE: 92 Degrees [from The Pop Will Eat Itself Cure For Sanity]
 ARTIST: Pop Will Eat Itself
COMMENT: "One of three tunes I did specially for demos by Rob Rawson Tetley
         (aka Demi-God). Unfortunately, he never got round to releasing
         anything else, so the tunes remain unused." (WP)

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/Aa_4_05_Preview_Version.sid
COMMENT: "An ambient tune I never finished because Kenz and I decided it didn't
         suit the Kenzterview." (WP)

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/Adventures_Of_Flopsy.sid
COMMENT: "A tune that was originally written for my Quadraped Tearaways 2 demo,
         but I rejected it due to its quite happy mood not suiting the rest of
         the tunes I wrote. I later donated it to a C64 user, Adam Langdon, for
         use with his programs." (WP)
         "The reason behind the title was Flopsy was the name of a cuddly bunny
         and he would feature in a really cutesy game. Unfortunately as I
         couldn't code this never took off." (WP)

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/Album_Sampler.sid
COMMENT: "The two tunes in this file are 7 inch edit versions of tracks later
         to appear on the 'Quadraped Tearaways' album demo." (WP)

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/Always_With_You.sid
COMMENT: "An exclusive tune I have done for News Press disk magazine, inspired
         by the fact that loved ones such as girlfriends or family pets are
         always with you in your mind even if you aren't actually with them.
         It's quite a nice plucked instrument on the C64 too, which took me
         ages to get right!" (WP)

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/Baby_Penguins_Adventure.sid
COMMENT: "I have a picture in my room which is a mother and father penguin,
         with its cute baby one in the middle. I just imagined the baby one
         having a little adventure, much like the cartoon series Pingu, and
         this made the theme in my head." (WP)
         "Another exclusive for Merman, this time for the 'Ferris Slideshow'
         and is really being cute in line with the Ferris character." (WP)

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/Beginnings.sid
  TITLE: Beginnings [from Midnight Lightning]
 ARTIST: Jimi Hendrix
COMMENT: "Only a part conversion of an obscure Jimi Hendrix track. My brother
         Clint is a huge fan of his music, and I was going to do a C64 demo
         with Hendrix cover versions in. It never took off." (WP)

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/Black_Sabbath.sid
  TITLE: Black Sabbath [from Black Sabbath]
 ARTIST: Black Sabbath
COMMENT: "My first ever tune using the Dutch USA Team's 'Music Assembler' and a
         request of another C64 user, Rory Stamp." (WP)

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/Blue_Monday_1991.sid
  TITLE: Blue Monday [from Substance 1987]
 ARTIST: New Order
COMMENT: "A severely remixed version of this single." (WP)

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/Bog_Swamp_Mix.sid

 ARTIST: BIGOD 20
COMMENT: "Jason MacKenzie (Kenz) likes this band very much, so I remixed this
         tune specially for use in 'The Kenzterview' demo." (WP)
         "Remix of song originally done by Industrial band BIGOD 20, who Kenz
         likes. So he asked me to do a version of one of their songs. This is

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/Death_By_The_Rack.sid

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/Desecrated_Mind.sid
  TITLE: Introfronty (0:00-0:50)
 ARTIST: Markus Merilainen
  TITLE: Introfronty (1:44-1:57)
 ARTIST: Markus Merilainen
  TITLE: Introfronty (3:19-3:35)
 ARTIST: Markus Merilainen
  TITLE: Introfronty (5:42)
 ARTIST: Markus Merilainen
COMMENT: "I wanted something hard for this, and after much listening to
         Obituary and Pantera, I had the idea - sound like Matt Gray with a
         mean bass and punchy drum. It sounds really aggressive and is the
         state of my mind due to my thoughts... and of course that tune
         'Introfronty' on the Amiga. I got that idea from listening to King
         Fisher's C64 version and thought 'that'd be cool to do with no digi
         drums." (WP)

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/Digital_Acid_Zaw_Remix.sid
  TITLE: Stakker Humanoid
 ARTIST: Humanoid
COMMENT: "This is basically a remixed version of the tune that Ash and Dave
         used on their 'Digital Acid' demo. I had found the locations in memory
         where they played all the samples in a certain order, and so I just
         changed those bytes around for a great new remix. All I then did was
         write a small machine code program to load the demo and place my remix
         data in the tune, and there you have it - Digital Acid remixed." (WP)

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/Distortion_Intentional.sid

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/Football_Tunes.sid
COMMENT: "This demo originally had references to my favourite club Manchester
         City but when I heard Thalamus were planning an Arsenal FC licence I
         changed the titles of tunes #2 and #7 to resemble Arsenal players or
         chants and sent it off to Dave Birch. I was going to do the music for
         that game but the project never saw the light of day!" (WP)
(#1)
  TITLE: The Stars and Stripes Forever
 ARTIST: John Philip Sousa
COMMENT: Often known to sports fans as "Here We Go".
(#2)
  TITLE: We All Follow the City
 ARTIST: <?>
COMMENT: Also known as 'Land Of Hope And Glory'.
(#3)
  TITLE: You're Going To Get Your F*****g Head Kicked In
 ARTIST: Traditional <?>
COMMENT: "A hooligan chant that surfaced rather nastily in the 80's, especially
         at West Ham, Millwall and Manchester Utd!" (WP)
(#4)
  TITLE: Blue Moon
 ARTIST: The Marcels
COMMENT: "Although a 50's song originally, Manchester City fans took it to
         their hearts around 1990 and made the song their own." (WP)
(#5)
  TITLE: When the Saints Go Marching In
 ARTIST: Traditional
COMMENT: American folk song. Sung by British football fans at matches.
         "An old community song, transformed by Souhampton FC into their
         original chant. Often used by other teams by substituting Saints with
         their club nickname." (WP)
(#6)
  TITLE: Saint And Greavsie

/VARIOUS/Zaw_Productions/Grandpa_Pacman.sid
COMMENT: "I was asked by a PC game programmer to do the music for the game. I
         composed it all on my C64, transferred it to SIDPlay, then the author
         converted the tracks to MIDI for use in the final version. Tunes #11
         and #12 were not used in the game. Shame, cos I tried to sound a
         little like Bjerregaard's jazz vibes in #12. If you want the game
         yourself to listen to how the MIDIs sounded, go to:

### MARTIN WALKER ############################################################

/Walker_Martin/Aggressor_Theme.sid
COMMENT: Also used in the game 'Revolution' (c) 1990 Commodore Format.

/Walker_Martin/Altered_Beast.sid
COMMENT: All the tunes cover the arcade version.

/Walker_Martin/Dragon_Breed.sid
COMMENT: All the tunes cover the arcade version.

/Walker_Martin/Galaxy_Force_II.sid
COMMENT: This could be a cover of the arcade version.

/Walker_Martin/Indiana_Jones_Fate_of_Atla.sid
  TITLE: Theme from The Raiders of the Lost Ark [from the movie]
 ARTIST: John Williams

/Walker_Martin/Ninja_Spirit.sid
COMMENT: Covers the arcade verison.

/Walker_Martin/Rodland.sid
COMMENT: All tunes come from the Amiga version of the game.

/Walker_Martin/Speedball_2.sid
  TITLE: SpeedBall 2
 ARTIST: Nation XII
COMMENT: Cover of the Amiga music.

/Walker_Martin/Walkers_Warbles.sid
COMMENT: "Walker's Warbles" was a Martin Walker music demo which came as a free
         giveaway with the disk version of "Armalyte" by Thalamus (which MW did
         the music for.) Tune 3 is an early version of what would eventually be
         used in the game "Dominion" bundled on a Zzap! 64 covertape in 1990,
         and tune 4 is an early version of the "Aggressor Theme". The final
         version of this (and the one which Revolution uses) is the one on MW's
         "Zzap! 64 Music Demo" which appeared on Zzap 64's June 1989 covermount
         cassette. (Comment by Warren.)
(#4)
COMMENT: This tune was also used in a game called Revolution (written by Simon
         Pick). The game was never released, but featured on Commodore Format
         magazine's powerpack (tape) number one.

         game. I did it after reading in some magazine about a possible C64
         conversion, and sent it Mirrorsoft "on spec" I don't know if the 64
         version was ever done, and if it was it didn't have my music in it."
         (MW)
         Actually, every conversion of Xenon 2 that had any music at all was
         covering this music.

### MICHAEL WINTERBERG #######################################################

/Winterberg_Michael/
COMMENT: "It's a long time ago that I composed sounds for the good old C64. But
         all I remember is that it was a very great time with much fun.
         My composing started in the years 1983/84 when I programmed my own
         music-editor and the music-play-routine. I made an extra-routine for
         each sound. From music to music I changed that routine to get
         different sounds.
         I have made some musics, good and bad sounds. But I have tried to make
         people happy with listening to my music." (Comment by Winterberg
         himself.)
